\Box\hat{\Psi}|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle=\bigg(\nabla^{2}\hat{\Psi}-\frac{1}{c^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\hat{\Psi}\bigg)|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle=0

(663)

where
c
is the speed of the qubit wave.

Next for the dual qubit wave, we have

\hat{\Psi^{\star}}(\theta)=\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})

(664)

By acting on the full state
|u\otimes u^{*}\rangle
,

\begin{aligned} \hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta)|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle&=\cos\theta(\hat{+}|u\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|u^{*}\rangle)+\sin\theta(\hat{-}|u\rangle\oplus\hat{+}_{3}|u^{*}\rangle)\\
&=\cos\theta(\hat{+}|0\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|1\rangle)+\sin\theta(\hat{-}|0\rangle\oplus\hat{+}|1\rangle)\\
&=\cos\theta|1\oplus 0\rangle+\sin\theta|0\oplus 1\rangle\end{aligned}\,,

(665)

We have

\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta)=\cos\theta|\text{creation}\rangle+\sin\theta|\text{annihilation}\rangle

(666)

where
|\text{creation}\rangle=*|\text{annihilation}\rangle
and
*|\text{creation}\rangle=|\text{annihilation}\rangle
.

P_{\text{creation}}(\theta)=\langle 1\oplus 0|\Psi^{\star}(\theta)\rangle=\cos^{2}\theta\,,

(667)

and

P_{\text{annihilation}}(\theta)=\langle 0\oplus 1|\Psi^{\star}(\theta)\rangle=\sin^{2}\theta\,.

(668)

In phase equal to zero, we have

|\Psi^{\star}(0)\rangle=|\text{creation}\rangle

(669)

with probability equal to 1 for creation state. And

|\Psi^{\star}(\pi/2)\rangle=|\text{annihilation}\rangle

(670)

with probability equal to 1 for the annihilation state. The two states are orthogonal,

\langle\Psi^{\star}(0)|\Psi^{\star}(\pi/2)\rangle=\langle\text{creation}|\text{annihilation}\rangle=0\,.

(671)

There is a special state when
\theta=\pi/2
, then we have the entangled EPR state as

|\Psi(\pi/4)\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|1\oplus 0\rangle+|0\oplus 1\rangle)

(672)

which is equal to

|\Psi(\pi/4)\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|\text{creation}\rangle+|\text{annihilation}\rangle)

(673)

Then the dual wave qubit operator is

\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(x,y,z,t)=\cos(\omega t-k_{x}x-k_{y}y-k_{z}z)(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin(\omega t-k_{x}x-k_{y}y-k_{z}z)(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\,.

(674)

By acting on the full state

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}^{\star}|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\cos(\omega t-k_{x}x-k_{y}y-k_{z}z)(\hat{+}|u\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|u^{*}\rangle)+\sin(\omega t-k_{x}x-k_{y}y-k_{z}z)(\hat{-}|u\rangle\oplus\hat{+}|u^{*}\rangle)

(675)

\displaystyle=\cos(\omega t-k_{x}x-k_{y}y-k_{z}z)|1\oplus 0\rangle+\sin(\omega t-k_{x}x-k_{y}y-k_{z}z)|0\oplus 1\rangle\,.

The dual qubit also satisfies the wave equation,

\Box\hat{\Psi}^{\star}|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle=\bigg(\nabla^{2}\hat{\Psi}^{\star}-\frac{1}{c^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\hat{\Psi}^{\star}\bigg)|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle=0\,.

(676)

The pair of states are orthogonal to each other

\langle\Psi(\theta)|\Psi^{\star}(\theta)\rangle=0\,.

(677)

Note that the linear combination of the two states is also the solution of the wave equation,

\phi=A\Psi+B\Psi^{\star}\,.

(678)

Therefore, when the dual states oscillate, it generates a wave propagation. For example, in equation
675
, the periodic cycle of creation and annihilation is a wave. Since mind creates and annihilates, this shows that mind is a wave. And for equation
662
, when the state of nothingness and All oscillate, it creates a wave. So the periodic transformation of nothingness and All will produce a wave signal, that maybe detected in experimental sense.

Next we would like to investigate what happens when the operators act on the dual of
|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle
, i.e.
*|u\oplus u^{*}\rangle=|u^{*}\oplus u\rangle
. Using the representation in
639
we find that the following holds,

\hat{P}*|0\rangle\oplus\hat{Q}*|1\rangle=\hat{P}|1\rangle\oplus\hat{Q}|0\rangle\,.

(679)

Explicitly, we can check that

\displaystyle\hat{-}*|0\rangle\oplus\hat{-}*|1\rangle

\displaystyle=\hat{-}|1\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|0\rangle=|0\rangle\oplus|0\rangle=\hat{-}|0\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|1\rangle\,,

(680)

\displaystyle\hat{+}*|0\rangle\oplus\hat{+}*|1\rangle

\displaystyle=\hat{+}|1\rangle\oplus\hat{+}|0\rangle=|1\rangle\oplus|1\rangle=\hat{+}|0\rangle\oplus\hat{+}|1\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\hat{+}*|0\rangle\oplus\hat{-}*|1\rangle

\displaystyle=\hat{+}|1\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|0\rangle=|0\rangle\oplus|1\rangle=\hat{+}|0\rangle\oplus\hat{-}|1\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\hat{-}*|0\rangle\oplus\hat{+}*|1\rangle

\displaystyle=\hat{-}|1\rangle\oplus\hat{+}|0\rangle=|1\rangle\oplus|0\rangle=\hat{-}|0\rangle\oplus\hat{+}|1\rangle\,.

Therefore, we have

\hat{-}*=\hat{-}\quad,\quad\hat{+}*=\hat{+}\,.

(681)

We can explicitly check that

\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\quad,\quad\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}\,.

(682)

Now we would like to give a more detailed study in the
\boldsymbol{M}
dual matrix operator. First notice that the action of
*=\boldsymbol{M}
swap the position of rows as a vertical reflection. For example, for a general matrix

A=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{then}\quad*A=\boldsymbol{M}A=A^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}c&d\\
a&b\end{pmatrix}\,.

(683)

If
A
acts on
\boldsymbol{M}
, the action is to swap the position of the columns as a horizontal reflection,

A*=A\boldsymbol{M}=\,^{*}A=\begin{pmatrix}b&a\\
d&c\end{pmatrix}\,.

(684)

Also we have

**A=A**=A\,.

(685)

We have the following identity, recalling
T
is the transpose action and
R
is the off-transpose action, we have

A^{(TR)}=A^{(RT)}=\boldsymbol{M}A\boldsymbol{M}^{-1}=\boldsymbol{M}A\boldsymbol{M}=*A*^{-1}=*A*\,,

(686)

as
\boldsymbol{M}^{-1}=\boldsymbol{M}
. It follows that
\mathrm{Tr}(A^{(TR)})=\mathrm{Tr}A
and
\mathrm{det}(A^{(TR)})=\mathrm{det}A
.
According to equations
681
,

*\hat{-}*=\hat{+}\quad,\quad*\hat{+}*=\hat{-}\,.

(687)

And as
*=*^{-1}
, thus
\hat{+}
and
\hat{-}
are related by similarity transform

*\hat{-}*^{-1}=\hat{+}\quad,\quad*\hat{+}*^{-1}=\hat{-}\,.

(688)

Next we would like to show that the identity

(*A*)^{-1}=*A^{-1}*\,.

(689)

This is because
(*A*)^{-1}=*^{-1}(*A)^{-1}=*A^{-1}*
.

Based on the result of
680
, if follows that
|u^{*}\oplus u\rangle
also satisfies the wave equation,

\Box\hat{\Psi}|u^{*}\oplus u\rangle=\bigg(\nabla^{2}\hat{\Psi}-\frac{1}{c^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\hat{\Psi}\bigg)|u^{*}\oplus u\rangle=0

(690)

and

\Box\hat{\Psi}^{\star}|u^{*}\oplus u\rangle=\bigg(\nabla^{2}\hat{\Psi}-\frac{1}{c^{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t^{2}}\hat{\Psi}^{\star}\bigg)|u^{*}\oplus u\rangle=0

(691)

Finally we will investigate the entropy of the system. The entropy for the both systems
|\Psi\rangle
and
|\Psi^{\star}\rangle
are

H(\theta)=-2\cos^{2}\theta\log|\cos\theta|-2\sin^{2}\theta\log|\sin\theta|\,.

(692)

When the probability is equal the entropy is maximized, thus the EPR state is the equilibrium state, which is the most stable state. The system has 0.693 bits. When it is either at its designated state with probability equal to 1, the entropy is zero, which is minimum and hence unstable.

10.3
Canonical Quantization

In this section, we will dive into the core part of the whole subject, the quantization of duality wave. Upon quantization, this will give the quanta of the duality field. The procedure is called second quantization, which is originated from quantum field theory. Second quantization turns a wave into a particle quanta.

First for the
\hat{\Phi}(\theta)
we define the conjugated momentum operator by

\Pi=\frac{h}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\Psi\,.

(693)

Therefore the conjugated momentum of
\Psi
is

\Pi(\theta)=\frac{h}{i}\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)\,.

(694)

Now we compute the commutator
[\hat{\Psi}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}(\theta)]
,

\displaystyle\left[\Psi(\theta),\Pi(\theta)\right]

\displaystyle=(-ih)[\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\,\,,\,\,-\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})]

(695)

\displaystyle=-ih\Big(-\cos\theta\sin\theta[(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}),(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})]-\sin^{2}\theta[(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}),(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})]

\displaystyle+\cos^{2}\theta[(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}),(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})]+\sin\theta\cos\theta[(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}),(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})]\Big)

\displaystyle=-ih[(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}),(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})]

\displaystyle=-ih\Big((\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})-(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})\Big)

\displaystyle=-ih(\hat{-}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{+}-\hat{+}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{-})

Now we take an ansatz for the replacement of
\hat{-},\hat{+}
matrix operators by bosonic creation and annihilation operators
\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}
with commutation relation of
[\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}]=1

\hat{-}\rightarrow\hat{a}^{\dagger}\quad,\quad\hat{+}\rightarrow\hat{a}

(696)

such that the quantum fields for duality are

\hat{\Psi}(\theta)=\cos\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\oplus\hat{a}^{\dagger})+\sin\theta(\hat{a}\oplus\hat{a})

(697)

and

\hat{\Pi}(\theta)=\frac{h}{i}\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\oplus\hat{a}^{\dagger})+\cos\theta(\hat{a}\oplus\hat{a})\Big)\,.

(698)

Then

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\Psi(\theta)},\hat{\Pi(\theta)}\right]

\displaystyle=-ih(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}\oplus\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}-\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\oplus\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger})

(699)

\displaystyle=-ih\bigg(\begin{pmatrix}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}&0\\
0&\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0\\
0&\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-ih\begin{pmatrix}-[\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}]&0\\
0&-[\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}]\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-ih\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=ih\boldsymbol{1}\,.

In the meantime, we have
\left[\hat{\Psi}(\theta),\hat{\Psi}(\theta)\right]=0
and
\left[\hat{\Pi}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}(\theta)\right]
. Therefore we have the canonical quantization condition as

\left[\hat{\Psi}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}(\theta)\right]=ih\boldsymbol{1}\quad,\quad\left[\hat{\Psi}(\theta),\hat{\Psi}(\theta)\right]=\left[\hat{\Pi}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}(\theta)\right]=0\,.

(700)

Next for the
\hat{\Phi}^{\star}(\theta)
we define the conjugated momentum operator by

\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta)=\frac{h}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\hat{\Psi}^{\star}\,.

(701)

Therefore the conjugated momentum of
\Psi
is

\Pi^{\star}(\theta)=\frac{h}{i}\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)\,.

(702)

Now we compute the commutator
[\hat{\Psi}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}(\theta)]
,

\displaystyle\left[\Psi^{\star}(\theta),\Pi^{\star}(\theta)\right]

\displaystyle=(-ih)\left[\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\,\,,\,\,-\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\right]

(703)

\displaystyle=-ih\Big(-\cos\theta\sin\theta\left[(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}),(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})\right]-\sin^{2}\theta\left[(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}),(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})\right]

\displaystyle+\cos^{2}\theta\left[(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}),(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\right]+\sin\theta\cos\theta\left[(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}),(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\right]\Big)

\displaystyle=-ih\left[(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}),(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\right]

\displaystyle=-ih\Big((\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})-(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})\Big)

\displaystyle=-ih(\hat{+}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{+}-\hat{-}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{-})

Then we have

\displaystyle\left[\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta)\right]

\displaystyle=-ih(\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\oplus\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}-\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}\oplus\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger})

(704)

\displaystyle=-ih\bigg(\begin{pmatrix}\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}&0\\
0&\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}&0\\
0&\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-ih\begin{pmatrix}[\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}]&0\\
0&-[\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}]\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=ih\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=ihM\,,

where
M
is another representation matrix of the duality operator with
M^{2}=\boldsymbol{1}
. In the meantime, we have
[\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta),\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta)]=0
and
[\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta)]=0
. Therefore we have the canonical quantization condition as

[\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta)]=ihM\quad,\quad[\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta),\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta)]=[\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta),\hat{\Pi}^{\star}(\theta)]=0\,.

(705)

Notice that the cross terms are not zero, this is given by the fact that the states are not independent of each other. For example

[\hat{\Psi}(\theta),\hat{\Psi}^{\star}(\theta)]=\begin{pmatrix}-\cos 2\theta&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\,.

(706)

10.3.1
Hamiltonian

The canonical momentum operator was defined by
\hat{\Pi}=\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
, the
h
is introduced such that we will get the same form of commutation relation as quantum mechanics does. From now on, to calculate the canonical Hamiltonian, let’s define the dimensionless conjugated momentum, still denoted as
\hat{\Pi}
,

\hat{\Pi}=\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\hat{\Psi}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{\Pi}^{\star}=\frac{1}{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\hat{\Psi}^{\star}

(707)

We ansatz that the Hamiltonian is defined as

\hat{H}=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Pi}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Psi}^{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{H}^{\star}=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Pi}^{\star 2}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Psi}^{\star 2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\,.

(708)

Now first we evaluate
\hat{H}(\theta)
.

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}^{2}(\theta)

(709)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i^{2}}\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})

\displaystyle-\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{-})-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{+})-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{+}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{-})+\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\bigg[(\sin^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\cos\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\cos\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}+\cos^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\sin^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})&0\\
0&\sin^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})\Big)\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\Big)\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}(2\sin\theta\cos\theta)\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}\Big)+\sin\theta\cos\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\boldsymbol{1}\,.

Then

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}^{2}(\theta)

(710)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)\Big(\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})

\displaystyle+\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{-})+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{+})+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{+}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{-})+\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg[(\cos^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}+\cos\theta\sin\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}+\cos\theta\sin\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}+\sin^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\cos^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})&0\\
0&\cos^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})\Big)\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})\Big)\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}\Big)\boldsymbol{1}+\frac{1}{2}(2\cos\theta\sin\theta)\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}\Big)\boldsymbol{1}+\sin\theta\cos\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\boldsymbol{1}\,.

Therefore, finally we obtain the Hamiltonian as follow

\hat{H}(\theta)=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Pi}^{2}(\theta)+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Psi}^{2}(\theta)\bigg)\hbar\omega=\frac{1}{2}\cos 2\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}+\sin 2\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

(711)

The expectation energy is hence given by

\displaystyle\langle E\rangle=\langle n|\hat{H}(\theta)|n\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cos 2\theta\langle n|(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})|n\rangle\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}+\sin 2\theta\langle n|\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)|n\rangle\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

(712)

\displaystyle=\sin 2\theta\bigg(n+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=H\sin 2\theta\,\boldsymbol{1}\,,

where

H=\bigg(n+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega.

(713)

This is because
\langle n|\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}|n\rangle=\langle n|\hat{a}\hat{a}|n\rangle=0
and
\langle n|\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}|n\rangle=n
. Thus this gives the oscillation of expectation of energy which depends upon the phase, which defines the quanta of duality wave, the dualiton. The vacuum expectation energy of the dualiton is given by

\langle 0|\hat{H}(\theta)|0\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}=\sin\theta\cos\theta\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(714)

Now let’s evaluate the vacuum expectation value for different important phases. When
\theta=0
or
\frac{\pi}{2}
, we have

E_{0}=\langle 0|\hat{H}(\theta)|0\rangle=\boldsymbol{0}\,.

(715)

Recalling that
\Psi(\theta)=\cos\theta|0\oplus 0\rangle+\sin\theta|1\oplus 1\rangle=\cos\theta|\text{nothingness}\rangle+\sin\theta|\text{All}\rangle
, this means when the dual wave function is at a particular state, then it has zero vacuum expectation energy value. On the other hand, when
\theta=\pi/4
,

E_{0}=\langle 0|\hat{H}(\theta)|0\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{2}\hbar\omega&0\\
0&\frac{1}{2}\hbar\omega\end{pmatrix}\,.

(716)

This means that when
\Psi(\frac{\pi}{4})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|0\oplus 0\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|1\oplus 1\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\text{nothingness}\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\text{All}\rangle
, the state is in linear superposition of the two states has its greatest entropy, it has the maximized energy. This shows that when the system is in its determined state of
|0\oplus 0\rangle
or
|1\oplus 1\rangle
it has lower ground state energy than the undetermined superpositioned state of
\Psi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|0\oplus 0\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|1\oplus 1\rangle
, thus is considered to be more stable. We say the superpositioned state is spontaneously collapsed into one of the state of full probability, this statement is equivalent to spontaneous symmetry breaking when a state is completely determined.

Next we evaluate
\hat{H}^{\star}(\theta)
.

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}^{\star 2}(\theta)

(717)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i^{2}}\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)\Big(-\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})

\displaystyle-\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{-})-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{+}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{+})-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{-})+\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\bigg[(\sin^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\cos\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\cos\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}+\cos^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\sin^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{+}\hat{-}+\hat{-}\hat{+})&0\\
0&\sin^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&0\\
0&\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}-\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)&0\\
0&\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}-\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\end{pmatrix}\,.

Then

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}^{\star 2}(\theta)

(718)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)\Big(\cos\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})

\displaystyle+\sin\theta\cos\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-})+\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})(\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\cos^{2}\theta(\hat{+}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{-})+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{+}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{-}\hat{+})+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{-})+\sin^{2}\theta(\hat{-}\hat{-}\oplus\hat{+}\hat{+})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg[(\cos^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}+\cos\theta\sin\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{+}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{-}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}+\cos\theta\sin\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{+}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{-}\end{pmatrix}+\sin^{2}\theta\begin{pmatrix}\hat{-}\hat{-}&0\\
0&\hat{+}\hat{+}\end{pmatrix}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\cos^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{+}\hat{-}+\hat{-}\hat{+})&0\\
0&\cos^{2}\theta\hat{-}\hat{-}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{+}\hat{+}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{-}\hat{+}+\hat{+}\hat{-})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a})&0\\
0&\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\cos\theta\sin\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger})\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)&0\\
0&\cos^{2}\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\sin^{2}\theta\hat{a}\hat{a}+\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\end{pmatrix}\,.

Therefore, finally we obtain the Hamiltonian as follow

\displaystyle\hat{H}^{\star}(\theta)=\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Pi}^{\star 2}(\theta)+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Psi}^{\star 2}(\theta)\bigg)\hbar\omega

(719)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{2}\cos 2\theta(\hat{a}\hat{a}-\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger})+\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)&0\\
0&\frac{1}{2}\cos 2\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})+\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\end{pmatrix}\hbar\omega

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cos 2\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})\hbar\omega M+\sin 2\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\,.

The expectation energy is hence given by

\displaystyle\langle E^{\star}\rangle=\langle n|\hat{H}^{\star}(\theta)|n\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cos 2\theta\langle n|(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})|n\rangle h\omega M+\sin 2\theta\langle n|\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)|n\rangle h\omega\boldsymbol{1}

(720)

\displaystyle=\sin 2\theta\bigg(n+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=H\sin 2\theta\,\boldsymbol{1}\,,

Therefore the expectation energy of
\Psi^{*}(\theta)
is same as the case of
\Psi(\theta)
. That means

\langle E(\theta)\rangle=\langle E^{\star}(\theta)\rangle=H\sin 2\theta\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(721)

Hence the phase analysis remains the same as the
\Psi(\theta)
case.

Figure 113
:
For the left figure, we compute the ground state
n=0
for phase
\theta=\pi/4,0,\pi/2
, corresponding to
|\Psi(\pi/4)\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|0\oplus 0\rangle+|1\oplus 1\rangle),|\Psi(0)\rangle=|0\oplus 0\rangle\,\,\text{and}\,\,|\Psi(\pi/2)\rangle=|1\oplus 1\rangle
respectively. The
|\Psi(\pi/4)\rangle
is a quantum state with probability of each state of
1/2
, it has the average ground state energy of
\frac{1}{2}\hbar\omega
, while
|\Psi(0)\rangle
and
|\Psi(\pi/2)\rangle
are classical states, with probability equal to 1 with average ground energy equal to zero. The idea is same for the right figure.

In the Hamiltonian, we have the extra term of
\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}
in addition to the standard Hamiltonian of simple harmonic oscillator. We would like to investigate its meaning here. First consider

\displaystyle(\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a})(\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a})

(722)

\displaystyle=\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}

\displaystyle=\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}+[\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}+1

Therefore

\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}=(\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a})(\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a})-1\,.

(723)

But recall that in quantum mechanics

\hat{x}=\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{2m\omega}}(\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a})\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{p}=i\sqrt{\frac{\hbar m\omega}{2}}(\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a})\,.

(724)

It follows that

\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}=\sqrt{\frac{2m\omega}{\hbar}}\hat{x}\cdot\frac{1}{i}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\hbar m\omega}}\hat{p}-1=\frac{2}{i\hbar}\hat{x}\hat{p}-1\,.

(725)

Therefore

\displaystyle\hat{H}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\cos 2\theta\bigg(\frac{1}{ih}\hat{x}\hat{p}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}+\sin 2\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

(726)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{i\hbar}\cos 2\theta\,\hat{x}\hat{p}+\sin 2\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}\bigg)\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}+\frac{1}{2}(\sin 2\theta-\cos 2\theta)\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=(\sin 2\theta\hat{n}-i\cos 2\theta\hat{\theta})\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}+\frac{1}{2}(\sin 2\theta-\cos 2\theta)\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}\,,

where

\hat{\theta}=\frac{\hat{x}\hat{p}}{\hbar}=\hat{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}

(727)

is the phase operator.

However, instead we can have

\displaystyle(\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a})(\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a})

(728)

\displaystyle=\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}-\hat{a}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}

\displaystyle=\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}+[\hat{a}^{\dagger},\hat{a}]

\displaystyle=\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}-1

Therefore

\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}=(\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a})(\hat{a}^{\dagger}+\hat{a})+1=\frac{2}{i\hbar}\hat{p}\hat{x}+1\,.

(729)

Then

\displaystyle\hat{H}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\cos 2\theta\bigg(\frac{1}{ih}\hat{p}\hat{x}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}+\sin 2\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

(730)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{i\hbar}\cos 2\theta\,\hat{p}\hat{x}+\sin 2\theta\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}\bigg)\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}+\frac{1}{2}(\sin 2\theta+\cos 2\theta)\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=(\sin 2\theta\hat{n}-i\cos 2\theta\hat{\theta}^{*})\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}+\frac{1}{2}(\sin 2\theta+\cos 2\theta)\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}\,,

where

\hat{\theta}^{*}=\frac{\hat{p}\hat{x}}{\hbar}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\hat{x}

(731)

is the dual phase operator.

Next we evaluate the phase expectation value. Consider

\langle n|\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}|n\rangle=0=\langle n|\frac{2}{i\hbar}\hat{x}\hat{p}-1|n\rangle=\frac{2}{i\hbar}\langle n|\hat{x}\hat{p}|n\rangle-\langle n|n\rangle=\frac{2}{i\hbar}\langle n|\hat{x}\hat{p}|n\rangle-1\\

(732)

Therefore we have

\langle n|\hat{\theta}|n\rangle=\frac{i}{2}\,.

(733)

On the other hand

\langle n|\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}|n\rangle=0=\langle n|\frac{2}{i\hbar}\hat{p}\hat{x}+1|n\rangle=\frac{2}{i\hbar}\langle n|\hat{x}\hat{p}|n\rangle+\langle n|n\rangle=\frac{2}{i\hbar}\langle n|\hat{x}\hat{p}|n\rangle+1\\

(734)

Therefore we have

\langle n|\hat{\theta}^{*}|n\rangle=\frac{-i}{2}\,.

(735)

Then we have

\langle n|\hat{\theta}+\hat{\theta}^{*}|n\rangle=0\,.

(736)

Thus we have the dual invariant operator as

\hat{\theta}+\hat{\theta}^{*}=\hat{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\hat{x}\,.

(737)

Therefore, we can see that
\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a}
relates to the dimensionless phase term, contrasting to then occupation number term
\hat{n}
.

From equation
726
, in particular at phase
\theta=\pi/8
, we have equal portion of
\hat{n}
and
\hat{\theta}
,

\hat{H}\Big(\frac{\pi}{8}\Big)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\hat{n}-i\hat{\theta})\hbar\omega\,\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(738)

We can see that at different phase, the Hamiltonian is dominated by different behaviour. At
\theta=\pi/4
, it is energy dominated,

H\Big(\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)=\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(739)

At
\theta=0
, it is phase dominated,

H(0)=\frac{1}{2}(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}=\bigg(-i\hat{\theta}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(740)

10.4
Partition function

In this section, we would study the partition function of the duality wave system. First, the phase dependent partition function is given by

Z(\theta)=\mathrm{Tr}\Big[\exp{\Big(-\beta\hat{H}(\theta)}\Big)\Big]\,.

(741)

For the
\hat{\Psi}(\theta)
field case, according to the Hamiltonian in
711
, it follows that

\displaystyle Z(\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\Big[\exp\Big(-\frac{\beta}{2}\cos 2\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}-\beta\sin 2\theta\Big(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\Big)\Big]

(742)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\langle n|\exp\Big(-\frac{\beta}{2}\cos 2\theta(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}^{\dagger}-\hat{a}\hat{a})\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}-\beta\sin 2\theta\bigg(\hat{a}^{\dagger}\hat{a}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg)\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\Big)|n\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\exp\Big(-\sin 2\theta\Big(n+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\Big)

The expectation energy is

\displaystyle\langle E(\theta)\rangle=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\ln Z(\theta)

(743)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\ln\Big(\sum_{n}\exp\Big(-\sin 2\theta\Big(n+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{n}\sin 2\theta\big(n+\frac{1}{2}\big)\hbar\omega\exp\Big(-\sin 2\theta\Big(n+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\Big)}{\sum_{n}\exp\Big(-\sin 2\theta\Big(n+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\big(-\frac{\sin 2\theta}{2}\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)\big[\sum_{n}n\hbar\omega\sin 2\theta\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)+\sum_{n}\frac{1}{2}\hbar\omega\sin 2\theta\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta\beta n\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)\big]}{\exp\big(-\frac{\sin 2\theta}{2}\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)\sum_{n}\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{n}n\hbar\omega\sin 2\theta\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}{\sum_{n}\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}+\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}

\displaystyle=\hbar\omega\sin 2\theta\frac{\sum_{n}n\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}{\sum_{n}\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}+\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\,.

Then let
X=\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)
, and using the identity,

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}X^{n}=\frac{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}-X}\,.

(744)

It follows that

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)=\frac{\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}-\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}\,.

(745)

Then using the identity of

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{d}{dX}X^{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{1}}{(\boldsymbol{1}-X)^{2}}

(746)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(X+2X^{2}+3X^{3}+\cdots+nX^{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{X}{(\boldsymbol{1}-X)^{2}}\,.

Therefore,

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)=\frac{\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}{\boldsymbol{1}-\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}\,.

(747)

Next

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum^{N}_{n=0}nX^{n}}{\sum_{n=0}^{N}X^{n}}=\frac{X}{1-X}\,.

(748)

Then

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sum_{n=0}^{N}n\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}{\sum_{n=0}^{N}\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}=\frac{\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}{\boldsymbol{1}-\exp\big(-\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)}=\frac{\boldsymbol{1}}{\exp\big(\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)-\boldsymbol{1}}\,.

(749)

Hence finally we obtain

\langle E(\theta)\rangle=\frac{\hbar\omega\sin 2\theta\boldsymbol{1}}{\exp\big(\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)-\boldsymbol{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(750)

Now we evaluate
\langle E(\theta)\rangle
in both high and low temperature limit. In high temperature limit
k_{B}T>>\hbar\omega
, we have

\langle E(\theta)\rangle\approx\frac{\hbar\omega\sin 2\theta\boldsymbol{1}}{\boldsymbol{1}+\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar{\omega}\boldsymbol{1}-\boldsymbol{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}=\frac{1}{\beta}=k_{B}T=\text{constant}\,,

(751)

which is independent of the phase
\theta
.

In low temperature limit
\hbar{\omega}>>k_{B}T
, or
T\rightarrow 0
,
\exp\big(\sin 2\theta n\beta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\big)\rightarrow\infty
, hence

\langle E(\theta)\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\sin 2\theta\hbar\omega\boldsymbol{1}\,.

(752)

This is just the same as the average ground state energy in
714
for
n=0
case. That means at
T=0K
, all states are at their ground state.

11
Conclusion

In this paper, we demonstrate how the fundamental concept of duality can be represented by rigorous mathematical and quantum physics formalism. The symmetry of duality and multi-duality are represented by the groups
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. The 4-duality group

\mathbb{Z}_{2}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
plays an important role in various dual structures, and can be represented by
a 4-tableau. The concepts of dual pairs and dual invariant numbers are also introduced. We have developed quantum field theory with duality and multi-duality symmetry and demonstrated how duality is embedded in the interaction terms. We have discovered that dual invariance implies topological property, for which the Chern-Simons action and the chiral anomaly action is a topological invariant. This profound result establishes a relation between dual invariance and topological invariance. Finally and most importantly, we exhibit how the oscillation of two dual states would produce a duality wave, and upon field quantization this produces a quasi-particle quanta called the dualiton. This is a new exotic particle produced by duality. A physical practice would be local continuous spin flip of an electron that produces a wave signal, and such wave is a particle quantum mechanically. This new particle is expected to have various contributions in particle physics and condensed matter physics.

There is no conflict of interest for this paper.
